Let's set up a computational plan in final code that actually searches and likely finds best. We can include multiple strategies:
1. Generate candidate families:
   - Uniform (score2)
   - Geometric r=1+c/n optimized c (score~1.806)
   - Power-law (k+1)^-0.5, binomial coefficients (score~1.57)
   - Polyalgo profiles: f_k = (k+1)^-p, p scan [0.4..0.5]
   - Smooth finite-profile optimization: represent f as sum of basis functions (powers, exponentials, splines) and use coordinate descent on coefficients.
2. Run local block search on best candidate of length n=4806 (or 4805) using exact evaluate_sequence.
   - Start from best family.
   - Use multiple random restarts/perturbations.
   - Use block multiplicative perturbations, coordinate descent on blocks, maybe every few blocks.
   - Use a "readjust" method: since scale invariant, after perturbations normalize? evaluate_sequence internally clips; we can normalize sequence to reasonable max (e.g., sum=1000) but score scale invariant. To avoid values >1000 or too small, scale candidate so max=1 or sum=1000.
   - Use timing to return best.

But if local search from binomial cannot beat π/2, our final maybe 1.57 > target. Need more aggressive search or better family.

Consider the continuous problem on [0,1] minimizing Q. We can use optimal control/Calculus of variations. Suppose optimum Q* and constraints active where convolution equals Q*. At optimum, by variational principle, for small variation δf, the maximum constraints active impose that the derivative of active conv is zero. If the active set includes an interval where conv=Q* (flat), then variation δ must satisfy ∫ δ(s)(f(m-s)+f? )? Let's design local search on full sequence more systematically using "convolution shaping":
- Compute current convolution b = np.convolve(f,f). Max M. The score =2nM/S^2.
- To improve, we can try to reduce max entries by lowering f at indices involved in high lags, and increase low entries (increase S) while not increasing max. High lags are positions where b near M. For each high lag m, the set of pairs (i,j) with i+j=m. Reducing f_i or f_j for i not too common can reduce b_m. Hard.
- A simple heuristic: If b has peak at central/right, reduce middle values; if peak at left, reduce early. But power binomial max left flat; tail slack.
- We can compute sensitivity: For changing f_i by δ, derivative of b_m = 2 f_{m-i} δ (for valid). For active max positions, to not increase, require sum over active m of 2 f_{m-i} δ ≤0. We can choose δ negative for indices that appear with large matching f in active positions. This is like water-filling.
- We can perform a small step in direction d where d_i=-1 for some critical indices, +1 for others, projected nonnegative. But computing active set may be useful.

Could implement a "smoothing" step: Convolution b = f*f. We want b flatter. In frequency domain, if b had negative sidelobes, but nonnegative. We can use iterative deconvolution? Maybe find f as square root of a target flat b. Given a target flat b (e.g., geometric or constant), solve f*f≈b via spectral methods (nonnegative deconvolution). For circular convolution, f can be inverse sqrt of b's FFT; take real, clip. For linear, zero-pad. This is a powerful constructive approach!

Idea: Choose a target convolution b_target that is flat (or has known low max) and try to find nonnegative f such that f*f ≈ b_target. If we can make f nonnegative, score good.Algorithm:
- Choose target b of length 2n-1, nonnegative, with small max relative to sum? We want b sum = S^2, max small. If b is flat constant C: sum=(2n-1)C, max=C. Then ideal S=√((2n-1)C), score=2n/(2n-1)≈1. We need nonnegative f whose square (linear convolution) approximates flat b.
- In frequency domain, for linear convolution, zero-pad f to length L≥2n-1. Let F = DFT(f). Want F^2 ≈ B (DFT of b). So F ≈ sqrt(B). Choose b_target, compute B, take principal square root with phase? B is real but can be negative at some frequencies; sqrt of negative gives imaginary. The time-domain f = IDFT(sqrt(B)) may have negative parts. We can choose phase (sign) of sqrt to make f nonnegative? This is phase retrieval. But we can take magnitude sqrt(|B|) and random phase, then optimize phase? Not trivial.

Iterative algorithm for nonnegative deconvolution to target b:
1. Initialize f nonnegative (e.g., ones).
2. Compute B_target (flat with edges maybe taper to be SOS).
3. Compute F current via FFT zero-padded.
4. Replace amplitude of F with sqrt(|B_target|) (or sqrt(B_target) complex if positive) while keeping phase from current F: F_new = phase(F) * sqrt(|B_target|).
5. f_new = real(IDFT(F_new)), clip negative, normalize.
This is a Griffin-Lim style algorithm to find f with specified b magnitude. It may converge to a nonnegative f approximating flat b! This could be very promising and efficient (FFT O(n log n)). We can implement custom iterative spectral shaping.

Need handle linear convolution. Let L = next power of two ≥2n (e.g., 16384 for n=4806). Zero-pad f length L, FFT F. The linear convolution c length 2n-1 is first 2n-1 of circular convolution of zero-padded f with itself, whose FFT is F^2. So if we set magnitude of F to sqrt(|B|) and phase, after IDFT and zero-padding, the square's first 2n-1 approximates target b.

```
def convolve_full(f):
    L=next_power_two(2*n)
    F=np.fft.rfft(f,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,L)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
This is much faster than direct and can be used for many iterations. Need ensure numerical errors nonnegative? Use float.

Let's implement a spectral reverse engineering with flat target and several tapers. It's novel and likely improves.

Detailed spectral function:
```
def next_pow2(x): return 1 << (int(x)-1).bit_length()
def fft_score(f, L, n):
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)[:2*n-1]
    S = f.sum()
    if S <=1e-9: return inf
    return 2*n*c.max()/S/S
```
For nonnegative f, c should be nonnegative. Due roundoff, max may be slightly off; okay.

Spectral shaping function:
```
def spectral_shape(base, n, target_mode, iters, rng, ...):
    L = next_pow2(2*n)
    # build target b length 2n-1
    m = np.arange(2*n-1)
    t = m/(2*n-2)
    if mode 'flat': target = np.ones_like(m)
    # optional edge taper: target *= np.maximum(0, 1 - edge_struct*(m - ...))? We want high sum so taper small.
    # For target with finite endpoints maybe target = np.linalg.inv? Use smooth: target = np.exp(-alpha * (np.minimum(m, 2n-2-m)/n)**2)
    B = np.fft.rfft(target, L)
    mag_target = np.sqrt(np.abs(B))
    f = base.copy().clip(0)
    if f.max()>0: f = f/f.max()
    best_f=f; best_score=fft_score(f,L,n)
    for it in range(iters):
       F = np.fft.rfft(f,L)
       mag = np.abs(F)
       # amplitude matching
       F_new = F * (mag_target / (mag + 1e-12))
       f2 = np.fft.irfft(F_new, L)[:n].real
       # maybe remove mean? no
       f2 = np.clip(f2,0,1000)
       if f2.max()>0: f2=f2/f2.max()
       # blend to avoid instability
       f = (1-tau)*f + tau*f2
       f = np.clip(f,0,1000)
       if f.max()>0: f=f/f.max()
       score=fft_score(f,L,n)
       if score<best_score: best=f.copy(); best_score=score
```
But amplitude matching to a fixed mag_target may not adjust sum/scale; scale invariant okay. However, target B computed from target of max1; its amplitude includes scale; if we normalize f max=1, the resulting f might not have correct relative scale? Scale doesn't matter for score; amplitude target absolute scale irrelevant because F_new scale set by mag_target, then we normalize. So okay.

Will this converge? It changes amplitude only, phase stays from f. Clipping changes phase. Could oscillate. Use multiple runs with different phases (random initial f).

We can also use exact square root phase update:
`F_des = np.sqrt(B + 1j*0)` (complex sqrt of target FFT). `f2 = ifft(F_des).real`. Clip. Blend. This imposes target phase, may produce positive? Try both variants.

- target = actual optimal? We can use flat target but set endpoints to avoid f0/fN constraints? Maybe target[m] = np.minimum(1, min(m, 2n-2-m)**0.5)? This makes edges increase from0, reducing max? Sum lower.

FFT score:
```
def fft_score(f, L, n):
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)
    c = c[:2*n-1]
    S = float(np.sum(f))
    if not finite or S <=1e-8: return np.inf
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if M < 0: M=0
    return 2.0*n*M/(S*S)
```
For f max1, S large, okay. c might have tiny negative; max positive.

Spectral target:
```
def make_target(n, mode, alpha=0.0):
    m = np.arange(2*n-1, dtype=np.float64)
    target = np.ones_like(m)
    gap = min(m, (2*n-2)-m)
    if mode=='exp':
       target *= np.exp(-alpha * (gap / max(1,n)))
    elif mode=='lin':
       g = max(1, int(n*alpha)) # if alpha fraction
       t = np.minimum(gap, g) / g
       target *= t*t? maybe linear t.
    elif mode=='sin':
       t = m/(2*n-2)
       target = 0.2 + 0.8*np.sin(np.pi*t)**alpha? alpha maybe 0.5
    return target
```
For flat, alpha=0. For sum preserve, maybe no normalization needed (scale target doesn't affect relative? Amplitude target scale affects F scale but final normalize, so target scale irrelevant! Because we normalize f after. Yes target absolute scale cancels. Only shape matters.

    return normalize_f(f)

Amplitude matching from a candidate:
```
def amplitude_match(f, target, L, n, iters, tau=0.5):
    B = np.fft.rfft(target, L)
    mag_target = np.sqrt(np.abs(B) + 1e-300)
    f = f.astype(float)
    best=f; best_score=fft_score(f,L,n)
    for it in range(iters):
       F = np.fft.rfft(f, L)
       mag = np.abs(F)
       ratio = mag_target / (mag + 1e-300)
       F2 = F * ratio
       f2 = np.fft.irfft(F2, L)[:n].real
       f2 = np.clip(f2,0,inf)
       if f2.max() <=0: break
       f2 = f2/f2.max()
       f = (1-tau)*f + tau*f2
       f = np.clip(f,0,inf)
       if f.max()>0: f=f/f.max()
       sc=fft_score(f,L,n)
       if sc < best_score:
           best=f.copy(); best_score=sc
    return best,best_score
```
This may drive amplitude to target but phase from current; if current phase poor, result poor. Use initial candidates: principal sqrt, and also base sequences. We can run amplitude_match from base for flat target; it will modify binomial's amplitude to flat target's amplitude, maybe improve.

We can also use "phase blending": F_new = F_current * exp(1j*0.5*angle(B)) * mag_target/|F|? That sets phase to half target and amplitude target, i.e., principal but with blend. Could implement as direct candidate.

Let's write functions:
```
def linear_conv_fft(f, L, n):
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)[:2*n-1].real
    return c

def flatten_shape(c, S, tau, floor_frac=0.05):
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if M <= 0: return c
    mean = S*S/(len(c))
    target = np.minimum(c, tau*M)
    target = np.maximum(target, floor_frac*mean)
    # Maybe shape smooth? target can have flat caps; okay.
    return target
```
If c has negative tiny, use np.maximum(c,0) for target.

Amplitude matching function can accept target shape and maybe `use_sqrt_phase`? We'll implement one that uses current phase.
```
def amplitude_match(f, target, L, n, iters, tau=0.5, max_iter...):
   B = np.fft.rfft(target, L)
   mag_target = np.sqrt(np.maximum(np.abs(B),0))
   best=f.copy(); best_sc=fft_score(f,L,n)
   for it in range(iters):
      F=np.fft.rfft(f,L)
      mag=np.abs(F)
      # avoid division zero: where mag==0, set F=0
      F2 = F * (mag_target / (mag + 1e-12 * (mag_target+1)))
      f2 = np.fft.irfft(F2,L)[:n].real
      f2 = np.clip(f2,0,1000)
      if f2.max()<=0: break
      f2 = f2/f2.max()
      f = (1-tau)*f + tau*f2
      f = np.clip(f,0,1000)
      if f.max()>0: f=f/f.max()
      sc=fft_score(f,L,n)
      if sc < best_sc:
         best=f.copy(); best_sc=sc
   return best, best_sc
```
The denominator epsilon should scale; use `den = np.maximum(np.abs(F), 1e-12)`.

   F=np.sqrt(B)
   f=np.fft.irfft(F,L)[:n].real
   f=np.clip(f,0,1000)
   return normalize_f(f), fft_score...

Adaptive flatten loop in propose:
```
def spectral_flatten(base, L, n, deadline, initial_tau=0.9):
   f=base.copy(); best=f; best_sc=fft_score(f,L,n)
   tau_decay=0.95
   outer=0
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
      c=linear_conv_fft(f,L,n)
      S=np.sum(f)
      target=flatten_shape(c,S,tau_decay)
      # run amplitude_match for some iters (e.g., 50)
      f_new, sc = amplitude_match(f, target, L,n, iters=50, tau=0.5, deadline?)
      if finite and < best_sc: best=f_new; best_sc=sc; f=f_new
      # also try direct candidate from target
      cand,_ = target_phase_candidate(target,L,n); if score ...
      # lower tau gradually to flatten more
      tau_decay=max(0.3, tau_decay*0.95)
   return best,best_sc
```

Pseudo:
```
def local_search(base, L,n,deadline):
   f=base.copy(); best=f; best_sc=fft_score(f,L,n)
   no=0
   amp_base=0.1
   while time<deadline:
      cand=f.copy()
      typ=rng.integers(0,5)
      if typ0 gaussian:
         w=int(rng.integers(5, max(10,n//5)))
         c=int(rng.integers(0,n-w//2?))... mask...
         amp=float(np.exp(rng.normal(np.log(0.1),0.5))) # 0.04-0.25
         cand *= np.exp(amp*mask)
      typ1 block:
         w=int(rng.integers(5,n//10)); start...
         cand[start:start+w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.2))
      typ2 reverse mix:
         t=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.3)); cand=(1-t)*f+t*f[::-1]; clip
      typ3 power:
         p=1+float(rng.normal(0,0.1)); cand=np.maximum(f,1e-6)**p; normalize
      typ4 endpoint tail boost:
         start=int(n*rng.uniform(0.3,0.8)); ramp=np.linspace(0,1,n-start); cand[start:]*=np.exp(0.1*ramp)
      cand=normalize_f(cand)
      sc=fft_score(cand,L,n)
      if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
          f=cand; best=cand; best_sc=sc; no=0
      else:
          no +=1
          if no % 500==0: amp_base*=??? or restart from best with big random
   return best,best_sc
```
Need normalize_f: clip, if max>0 divide max. If all zero, return base.
Power transform with very small values can underflow; add eps: `np.power(np.maximum(f,1e-6), p)`.
Reverse mix may produce symmetric-like with worse score, but small t maybe.

Normalize function:
```
def normalize_array(f):
    arr = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.zeros_like(arr)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    mx = float(np.max(arr))
    if mx > 1e-12:
        arr = arr/mx
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if s < 1e-6:
        arr[:] = 1.0
    return arr
```
Should we normalize max to 1? Yes. For uniform, max1. Sum large. Score invariant. If we scale max1 some values tiny but >0. Good.

def exact_score_list(seq_list):
    try:
       s=evaluate_sequence(seq_list)
       return float(s)
    except Exception:
       return np.inf
```
No import.

def target_sqrt_candidate(target, L, n):
    B = np.fft.rfft(target, L).astype(np.complex128)
    # Ensure nonnegative DC? sqrt principal. 
    F = np.sqrt(B)
    f = np.fft.irfft(F, L)[:n].real
    return normalize_array(f)
```
But if B has imaginary numerical, sqrt okay. For bins where B very small negative real, principal gives imaginary, f oscillatory. Okay.

Adaptive target flatten:
```
def make_flattened_target(c, S, tau):
    cpos = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    M = float(np.max(cpos))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0:
        return np.ones_like(cpos)
    mean = (S*S)/float(cpos.size)
    if mean <=0: mean=M
    target = np.minimum(cpos, tau*M)
    floor = 0.02*mean
    target = np.maximum(target, floor)
    # smooth target a bit? Could np.convolve with small kernel; not needed.
    return target
```
If tau small, target mostly floor/tau, still positive.

Amplitude match function might need `deadline` to stop:
```
def amplitude_match(f, target, L, n, iters, tau_blend, deadline):
    B = np.fft.rfft(target, L)
    mag_target = np.sqrt(np.abs(B))
    best = f.copy(); best_sc = fft_score(f,L,n)
    for it in range(iters):
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       F = np.fft.rfft(f, L)
       mag = np.abs(F)
       den = np.maximum(mag, 1e-12)
       F2 = F * (mag_target / den)
       f2 = np.fft.irfft(F2, L)[:n].real
       f2 = np.clip(f2, 0.0, 1000.0)
       mx = float(np.max(f2))
       if mx <= 1e-12: break
       f2 = f2/mx
       f = (1.0 - tau_blend)*f + tau_blend*f2
       f = np.clip(f,0.0,1000.0)
       mx=float(np.max(f));
       if mx>1e-12: f=f/mx
       sc=fft_score(f,L,n)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
           best = f.copy(); best_sc=sc
    return best,best_sc
```
Potential overflow in mag_target/den if mag_target large (target sum scale). But we normalize target? target values maybe S^2 ~n^2? For f max1, sum ~√n for binomial, S^2~n, target max~n? B[0]~M*2n ~n^2? mag_target ~n. den initially maybe |F[0]|=S~√n, ratio ~√n, F2 magnitude becomes n? Inverse f2 scale maybe? Then normalize. Ratio can be large but finite. To keep numbers stable, normalize target before FFT? Since target scale irrelevant, we can scale target to max=1 before rfft. Yes! In amplitude_match, do `target = target / max(target)` before B. Then mag_target O(√L). Candidate scale then normalize. Great. Also flatten target can be scaled to max1. Implement in `amplitude_match`: `tmax=np.max(target); if tmax>0: target=target/tmax` (copy maybe). This avoids large.
Similarly direct target candidate: scale target max1 (not necessary).
For adaptive target, we can scale.

FFT score uses f max1, no scaling issue.

`target = target / max(target)`.

We can use a manual insert function with loops (no lambda).
```
def add_candidate(pool, score, f, limit=50):
   if not np.isfinite(score): return
   arr=f.copy()
   if len(pool)>=limit:
       max_idx=0
       for i in range(1,len(pool)):
          if pool[i][0] > pool[max_idx][0]: max_idx=i
       if score >= pool[max_idx][0]: return
       pool.pop(max_idx)
   pool.append((score, arr))
```
Pool entries tuple (float, array); only compare floats manually.

Large random perturbation for restart:
```
def random_big_perturb(f, rng, n):
   c=f.copy()
   # multiply by exp(normal(0,0.5,n)) and clip, also random power
   noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.5,size=n))
   c *= noise
   p = float(rng.uniform(0.5,1.5))
   c = np.power(np.maximum(c,1e-8),p)
   return normalize_array(c)
```

Let's add one more candidate generation: "square root of flat target with random phase signs". Function `random_sign_target_candidate`:
```
def signed_target_candidate(target,L,n,rng,frac_neg):
   B=np.fft.rfft(target,L).astype(np.complex128)
   half=L//2
   signs=np.ones(half+1, dtype=float)
   for k in range(1,half):
      if rng.random() < frac_neg: signs[k] = -1.0
   B = B * signs
   F=np.sqrt(B)
   f=np.fft.irfft(F,L)[:n].real
   return normalize_array(f)
```
But as noted, random signs change target spectrum; may produce candidates with actual conv not target. Still could be diverse. We can generate a few for flat target.
Need signs for Nyquist fixed1. The full FFT with negative signs is Hermitian? Since rfft modified with real signs for positive frequencies; negative counterparts implicitly conjugated. If B[k] * (-1) and B[-k]=conj(B[k]), then B[-k]*(-1)=conj(-B[k])? sign real same, yes. Good.
Random signs may yield f with negative values; clip.

def resize_array(x, n):
    x=np.asarray(x,float).clip(0,1000)
    if x.size==0: return np.ones(n)
    if x.size==n: return normalize(x)
    old=np.linspace(0,1,len(x))
    new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    return normalize(np.interp(new, old, x))
```

`build_base(n, kind, rng)`:
```
def make_binomial(n):
    f=np.empty(n,float); f[0]=1.0
    for k in range(n-1): f[k+1]=f[k]*(2*k+1)/(2*k+2)
    return f
```
Wait recurrence: for k from 0 to n-2, a_{k+1}=a_k*(2*k+1)/(2*k+2). For k=0:1/2, k=1:3/4. Good.
`power`: `np.power(np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float), -0.5)`
`geometric`: t=(np.arange(n))/(n-1); c=1.2; f=np.exp(c*t); normalize.
`uniform`: ones.
`sine`: `np.sin(np.pi*np.arange(n)/(n-1))` (endpoints zero except last? sin pi=1.22e-16, okay) normalize (max1).

`run_spectral(f,L,n,deadline)`:

Pseudo:
```
def run_spectral(f, L, n, deadline):
    f=normalize_array(f)
    best=f.copy(); best_sc=fft_score(f,L,n)
    # fixed flat
    if time.monotonic()<deadline:
        target=np.ones(2*n-1)
        sc=fft_score(target_sqrt_candidate(target,L,n),L,n); update
        fnew,sc=amplitude_match(f,target,L,n,iters=max(50,...),tau=0.6,deadline=min(deadline, time+20))
        update
    tau=0.95
    no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c=linear_conv_fft(best,L,n)
        S=float(np.sum(best))
        target=make_flattened_target(c,S,tau)
        # direct
        cand=target_sqrt_candidate(target,L,n); sc=fft_score(cand,L,n); update
        iters = 80 if best_sc < 1.6 else 40
        fnew,sc = amplitude_match(best,target,L,n,iters=iters,tau_blend=0.6,deadline=min(deadline, time+30))
        if sc<best_sc:
            best=fnew; best_sc=sc; no_improve=0
        else:
            no_improve +=1
        tau = max(0.25, tau*0.85)
        if no_improve > 10 or tau <0.26:
            break
    return best,best_sc
```
Use `np.sum(best)` not exact S? Yes.
The amplitude_match returns best within itself; we pass current best.
The deadline min inside uses `time.monotonic() + 30.0` but if deadline sooner.
Potential: run_spectral may run too long if deadline far and no improvement; tau decreases and break.

`run_local_search(f,L,n,deadline)`:
```
def run_local_search(f,L,n,deadline,rng):
   f=normalize_array(f); best=f; best_sc=fft_score(f,L,n)
   no=0
   while time<deadline:
      typ=int(rng.integers(0,6))
      cand=f.copy()
      if typ==0: gaussian...
      elif typ==1: block...
      elif typ==2: tail boost...
      elif typ==3: power...
      elif typ==4: reverse mix...
      else: random smooth all...
      cand=normalize_array(cand)
      sc=fft_score(cand,L,n)
      if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
          best=cand; best_sc=sc; no=0
      else:
          no+=1
          if no > 2000:
             # restart with big perturbation
             cand_big=random_big_perturb(best,rng,n); sc=fft_score...
             if sc < best_sc: best=cand... else maybe break? set no=0
   return best,best_sc
```
Need not restart too often; if no > 5000 maybe break? Use time.
Gaussian mask:
```
w=int(rng.integers(7, max(8, n//4)))
if w>n: w=n
center=int(rng.integers(0,n))
left=max(0,center-w); right=min(n,center+w)
if right-left<5: continue
idx=np.arange(left,right)
mask=np.exp(-0.5*((idx-center)/(0.5*w))**2)
amp=float(np.exp(rng.normal(np.log(0.15),0.6))) # 0.06-0.4
cand[left:right] *= np.exp(amp*mask)
```
Block:
```
w=int(rng.integers(5,max(6,n//20))); start=int(rng.integers(0,n-w)); factor=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.15)); cand[start:start+w]*=factor
```
Tail boost: start = int(n*rng.uniform(0.25,0.8)); slope=np.arange(n-start)/(n-start+1); cand[start:]*=np.exp(0.2*slope)
Power: p=1+float(rng.normal(0,0.12)); cand=np.power(np.maximum(f,1e-6),p)
Reverse mix: t=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.25)); cand=(1-t)*f + t*f[::-1]
Global smooth: cand = f * exp(rng.normal(0,0.05,n))
```
After perturb, normalize.
If candidate all zeros, normalize returns ones? Good.

Let's construct pool sorting function without lambda:
```
def sort_pool(pool):
    # use insertion sort small
    for i in range(1,len(pool)):
        item=pool[i]
        j=i-1
        while j>=0 and pool[j][0] > item[0]:
            pool[j+1]=pool[j]
            j-=1
        pool[j+1]=item
```

If B[k] negative real, conjugate B[-k] also negative real (since real), principal sqrt would be i√x for both, not conjugate (should be -i for negative). This breaks Hermitian, irfft may produce small imaginary? np.fft.irfft assumes input's counterpart is conjugate; if not, it may produce incorrect? The rfft input only positive; irfft internally assumes symmetry with conjugate. If we provide sqrt where symmetry broken at negative real bins, result may be garbage at those bins. Better to enforce a consistent phase choice for real f: for each frequency bin k, choose phase = 0.5*angle(B[k]) but for negative real B (angle=π), principal gives π/2; the negative bin would need -π/2. irfft doesn't know negative. It assumes the positive frequency phasor's conjugate is at negative; if positive is i√x, conjugate is -i√x, corresponding to phase -π/2, which is not the principal sqrt of the negative bin but that's okay because rfft only stores positive; the imaginary part sign determines symmetry? irfft of a positive frequency with phase π/2 and its implicit conjugate phase -π/2 is fine. It doesn't require principal sqrt of negative bin. So okay.
But if B[k]=0, sqrt0.

For random signs, multiplying B by -1 for a complex bin changes angle by π; sqrt angle half = original/2 + π/2. The implicit conjugate uses negative of that, consistent. Good.

In `amplitude_match`, we preserve F phase from current f, which is Hermitian, so irfft safe.

If f real, F is rfft; F*F is the rfft of circular convolution? For each positive k, product of positive frequencies; the corresponding negative frequency is conjugate (conj(F) * conj(F) = conj(F^2)), so irfft okay. Good.

def run_spectral(f,L,n,deadline,rng):
   f=normalize_array(f); best=f.copy(); best_sc=fft_score(f,L,n)
   if time.monotonic() >= deadline: return best,best_sc
   target_flat=np.ones(2*n-1, dtype=float)
   for _ in range(3):
      cand=target_sqrt_candidate(target_flat,L,n); sc=fft_score(cand,L,n); update
      if time>=deadline: break
   fixed_deadline=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+30.0)
   fnew,sc=amplitude_match(best,target_flat,L,n,iters=300,tau_blend=0.6,deadline=fixed_deadline)
   update
   tau=0.95
   stable=0
   while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       c=linear_conv_fft(best,L,n)
       S=float(np.sum(best))
       target=make_flattened_target(c,S,tau)
       cand=target_sqrt_candidate(target,L,n); sc=... update
       inner_deadline=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+25.0)
       fnew,sc=amplitude_match(best,target,L,n,iters=200,tau_blend=0.6,deadline=inner_deadline)
       if finite and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
           best=fnew.copy(); best_sc=sc; stable=0
       else:
           stable +=1
       tau=max(0.2,tau*0.85)
       if stable>6 or tau <0.21: break
   return best,best_sc
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    budget=max(0.0,float(budget_s))
    if budget <=0: return [1.0]
    reserve = 60.0 if budget>=1200 else 15.0
    search_deadline = start + max(0.0, budget - reserve)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # choose n
    if budget >= 900: n=8191
    elif budget >= 240: n=4095
    else: n=2047
    L=next_power_of_two(2*n)
    if L < 2*n: L *=2
    pool=[]
    # bases
    for kind...
    # height_sequence_1 if exists
    hs = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    if hs is not None:
       try:
          arr=np.asarray(hs,float).copy()
          arr=np.nan_to_num(arr,...)
          if arr.size>0:
             if arr.size != n: arr=np.interp(np.linspace(0,1,n), np.linspace(0,1,arr.size), arr)
             c=normalize_array(arr); sc=fft_score(c,L,n); add_candidate
       except: pass
    # flat direct and signed
    target_flat=np.ones(2*n-1)
    add_candidate(pool, fft_score(target_sqrt_candidate(...),L,n), ...)
    for frac in (0.1,0.3,0.6): add signed
    sort_pool(pool)
    best_seq = pool[0][1].copy() if pool else np.ones(n)
    best_fft = pool[0][0] if pool else inf
    # rounds
    n_rounds = 10
    for r in range(n_rounds):
       if time.monotonic() >= search_deadline - 30: break
       # choose start
       if len(pool)>r+1:
           start_seq = pool[r+1][1].copy()
       else:
           start_seq = best_seq.copy()
       # occasionally perturb
       if r>0 and rng.random()<0.5:
           start_seq = random_big_perturb(start_seq,rng,n)
       # time slice length ~ min(120, remaining/ (n_rounds-r))
       remaining = search_deadline - time.monotonic()
       if remaining <=0: break
       slice_len = min(150.0, max(30.0, remaining / max(1, n_rounds - r)))
       spec_end = start_time + slice_len*0.4
       loc_end = start_time + slice_len
       # ensure not beyond search_deadline
       spec_end = min(spec_end, search_deadline)
       loc_end = min(loc_end, search_deadline)
       f1, sc1 = run_spectral(start_seq,L,n,spec_end,rng)
       if finite and sc1 < best_fft: best_seq=f1; best_fft=sc1
       add_candidate(pool, sc1, f1)
       f2, sc2 = run_local_search(f1,L,n,loc_end,rng)
       if finite and sc2 < best_fft: best_seq=f2; best_fft=sc2
       add_candidate(pool, sc2, f2)
       # Also local on best_seq maybe? maybe not
    sort_pool(pool)
    # exact eval
    exact_best=None; exact_best_score=np.inf
    end_time=start+budget
    idxs=list(range(len(pool)))
    sort_pool creates sorted; use pool first 8.
    for i in range(min(8,len(pool))):
       if time.monotonic() >= end_time - 1.0: break
       seq=[float(x) for x in pool[i][1]]
       try: sc=evaluate_sequence(seq)
       except Exception: sc=np.inf
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc<exact_best_score:
           exact_best_score=sc; exact_best=seq
    # also evaluate best_seq if not in pool or exact not yet?
    seq=[float(x) for x in best_seq]
    if time.monotonic() < end_time-1:
       sc=evaluate_sequence(seq) update
    if exact_best is None:
       exact_best=seq
    return exact_best

def resize_array(x, n):

Now, final answer must be one fenced Python code block, no extra text. Let's produce.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2 * n * max(conv(f,f)) / (sum(f))**2.

The main ideas are:
- Start from several analytically motivated nonsymmetric profiles
  (binomial coefficients, power-law, exponential, uniform, sine, etc.).
- Use FFT-accelerated linear convolution for fast scoring during search.
- Use a spectral inverse-engineering step: try to shape f so that its
  convolution matches a flattened target convolution.
- Use adaptive peak-flattening targets built from the current convolution.
- Finish with smooth multiplicative/parametric local search.
- Evaluate only a small number of final candidates with the exact
  evaluate_sequence() routine and return the best exact candidate.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def next_power_of_two(x):
    x = int(x)
    if x <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_array(f):
    arr = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return arr
    mx = float(np.max(arr))
    if mx <= 1e-14:
        arr[:] = 1.0
        return arr
    arr = arr / mx
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(np.sum(arr)) < 1e-6:
        arr[:] = 1.0
    return arr


def resize_array(x, n):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == n:
        return normalize_array(x)
    old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, x.size, dtype=np.float64)
    new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    y = np.interp(new, old, x)
    return normalize_array(y)


def make_binomial_profile(n):
    f = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    f[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(n - 1):
        f[k + 1] = f[k] * (2.0 * k + 1.0) / (2.0 * k + 2.0)
    return normalize_array(f)


def fft_score(f, L, n):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    if f.size != n:
        return np.inf
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(f)):
        return np.inf
    S = float(np.sum(f))
    if not np.isfinite(S) or S <= 1e-8:
        return np.inf
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)[:2 * n - 1].real
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M < 0.0:
        M = 0.0
    return 2.0 * float(n) * M / (S * S)


def linear_conv_fft(f, L, n):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)[:2 * n - 1].real
    return c


def make_flattened_target(c, S, tau):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.nan_to_num(c, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    cpos = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    M = float(np.max(cpos))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
        return np.ones_like(cpos, dtype=np.float64)
    mean = (S * S) / float(cpos.size)
    if not np.isfinite(mean) or mean <= 0.0:
        mean = M
    target = np.minimum(cpos, tau * M)
    floor = 0.02 * mean
    target = np.maximum(target, floor)
    mx = float(np.max(target))
    if mx > 0.0 and np.isfinite(mx):
        target = target / mx
    return target


def target_sqrt_candidate(target, L, n):
    target = np.asarray(target, dtype=np.float64)
    tx = float(np.max(target))
    if tx > 0.0 and np.isfinite(tx):
        target = target / tx
    B = np.fft.rfft(target, L).astype(np.complex128)
    F = np.sqrt(B)
    f = np.fft.irfft(F, L)[:n].real
    return normalize_array(f)


def signed_target_candidate(target, L, n, rng, frac_neg):
    target = np.asarray(target, dtype=np.float64)
    tx = float(np.max(target))
    if tx > 0.0 and np.isfinite(tx):
        target = target / tx
    B = np.fft.rfft(target, L).astype(np.complex128)
    half = L // 2
    signs = np.ones(half + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(1, half):
        if rng.random() < frac_neg:
            signs[k] = -1.0
    B = B * signs
    F = np.sqrt(B)
    f = np.fft.irfft(F, L)[:n].real
    return normalize_array(f)


def add_candidate(pool, score, f, limit=80):
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    arr = normalize_array(f)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return
    if len(pool) >= limit:
        worst_idx = 0
        for i in range(1, len(pool)):
            if pool[i][0] > pool[worst_idx][0]:
                worst_idx = i
        if score >= pool[worst_idx][0]:
            return
        pool.pop(worst_idx)
    pool.append((float(score), arr.copy()))


def sort_pool(pool):
    for i in range(1, len(pool)):
        item = pool[i]
        j = i - 1
        while j >= 0 and pool[j][0] > item[0]:
            pool[j + 1] = pool[j]
            j -= 1
        pool[j + 1] = item


def amplitude_match(f, target, L, n, iters, tau_blend, deadline):
    f = normalize_array(f)
    target = np.asarray(target, dtype=np.float64)
    target = np.nan_to_num(target, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    target = np.maximum(target, 0.0)
    tx = float(np.max(target))
    if tx > 0.0 and np.isfinite(tx):
        target = target / tx

    B = np.fft.rfft(target, L).astype(np.complex128)
    mag_target = np.sqrt(np.abs(B))

    best = f.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(f, L, n)

    for _ in range(int(max(0, iters))):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        F = np.fft.rfft(f, L)
        mag = np.abs(F)
        den = np.maximum(mag, 1e-12)
        ratio = np.clip(mag_target / den, 0.0, 1e6)
        F2 = F * ratio
        f2 = np.fft.irfft(F2, L)[:n].real
        f2 = np.clip(f2, 0.0, 1000.0)
        f2 = normalize_array(f2)
        f = (1.0 - tau_blend) * f + tau_blend * f2
        f = normalize_array(f)
        sc = fft_score(f, L, n)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best = f.copy()
            best_score = sc

    return best, best_score


def run_spectral(f, L, n, deadline, rng):
    f = normalize_array(f)
    best = f.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(f, L, n)

    if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
        return best, best_score

    target_flat = np.ones(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)

    cand = target_sqrt_candidate(target_flat, L, n)
    sc = fft_score(cand, L, n)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        best = cand.copy()
        best_score = sc

    fixed_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 25.0)
    cand, sc = amplitude_match(best, target_flat, L, n, 250, 0.6, fixed_deadline)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        best = cand.copy()
        best_score = sc

    tau = 0.95
    stable = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = linear_conv_fft(best, L, n)
        S = float(np.sum(best))
        target = make_flattened_target(c, S, tau)

        cand = target_sqrt_candidate(target, L, n)
        sc = fft_score(cand, L, n)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best = cand.copy()
            best_score = sc
            stable = 0

        inner_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 20.0)
        cand, sc = amplitude_match(best, target, L, n, 180, 0.6, inner_deadline)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = cand.copy()
            best_score = sc
            stable = 0
        else:
            stable += 1

        tau = max(0.20, tau * 0.85)
        if stable > 8 or tau < 0.205:
            break

    return best, best_score


def random_big_perturb(f, rng, n):
    f = normalize_array(f)
    out = f.copy()
    noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, size=n))
    out *= noise
    p = float(rng.uniform(0.55, 1.45))
    out = np.power(np.maximum(out, 1e-8), p)
    return normalize_array(out)


def run_local_search(f, L, n, deadline, rng):
    f = normalize_array(f)
    best = f.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(f, L, n)
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        typ = int(rng.integers(0, 7))
        cand = best.copy()

        if typ == 0:
            w = int(rng.integers(7, max(8, n // 4)))
            if w > n:
                w = n
            center = int(rng.integers(0, n))
            left = max(0, center - w)
            right = min(n, center + w)
            if right - left >= 7:
                idx = np.arange(left, right, dtype=np.float64)
                mask = np.exp(-0.5 * ((idx - center) / (0.5 * w)) ** 2)
                amp = float(np.exp(rng.normal(np.log(0.15), 0.6)))
                cand[left:right] *= np.exp(amp * mask)

        elif typ == 1:
            w = int(rng.integers(5, max(6, n // 18)))
            if w > n:
                w = n
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - w + 1)))
            factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.18)))
            cand[start:start + w] *= factor

        elif typ == 2:
            start = int(n * float(rng.uniform(0.25, 0.80)))
            if start < n:
                slope = np.arange(n - start, dtype=np.float64)
                slope = slope / (slope.size + 1.0)
                cand[start:] *= np.exp(0.22 * slope)

        elif typ == 3:
            p = 1.0 + float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.10))
            cand = np.power(np.maximum(best, 1e-8), p)

        elif typ == 4:
            t = float(rng.uniform(0.03, 0.22))
            cand = (1.0 - t) * best + t * best[::-1]

        elif typ == 5:
            cand *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, size=n))

        else:
            w = int(rng.integers(3, max(4, n // 50)))
            for _ in range(3):
                start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - w + 1)))
                cand[start:start + w] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.12)))

        cand = normalize_array(cand)
        sc = fft_score(cand, L, n)

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = cand
            best_score = sc
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 2500:
                cand2 = random_big_perturb(best, rng, n)
                sc2 = fft_score(cand2, L, n)
                if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score:
                    best = cand2
                    best_score = sc2
                    no_improve = 0
                elif no_improve > 9000:
                    no_improve = 0

    return best, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    if budget <= 0.0:
        return [1.0]

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    if budget >= 900:
        n = 8191
    elif budget >= 240:
        n = 4095
    else:
        n = 2047

    L = next_power_of_two(2 * n)
    if L < 2 * n:
        L *= 2

    reserve = 75.0 if n > 4000 else 25.0
    search_deadline = start_time + max(0.0, budget - reserve)

    pool = []

    # Basic analytical candidates.
    cand = make_binomial_profile(n)
    add_candidate(pool, fft_score(cand, L, n), cand)

    cand = make_binomial_profile(n)[::-1]
    add_candidate(pool, fft_score(cand, L, n), cand)

    f = np.power(np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64), -0.5)
    add_candidate(pool, fft_score(normalize_array(f), L, n), f)

    t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, n - 1))
    for c in (0.8, 1.2, 1.6):
        f = np.exp(c * t)
        add_candidate(pool, fft_score(f, L, n), f)

    f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    add_candidate(pool, fft_score(f, L, n), f)

    f = np.sin(np.pi * t)
    add_candidate(pool, fft_score(f, L, n), f)

    f = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.35, size=n)) + 0.15
    add_candidate(pool, fft_score(f, L, n), f)

    # Use the known previous construction if available.
    hs = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    if hs is not None:
        try:
            arr = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
            if arr.size > 0:
                arr = resize_array(arr, n)
                add_candidate(pool, fft_score(arr, L, n), arr)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Direct inverse-spectral candidates from a flat target.
    target_flat = np.ones(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cand = target_sqrt_candidate(target_flat, L, n)
    add_candidate(pool, fft_score(cand, L, n), cand)

    for frac in (0.10, 0.25, 0.45):
        cand = signed_target_candidate(target_flat, L, n, rng, frac)
        add_candidate(pool, fft_score(cand, L, n), cand)

    sort_pool(pool)
    if len(pool) == 0:
        best_seq = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        best_fft = np.inf
    else:
        best_seq = pool[0][1].copy()
        best_fft = pool[0][0]

    print("n=%d L=%d initial_best_fft=%.9f" % (n, L, best_fft), flush=True)

    n_rounds = 12
    for r in range(n_rounds):
        if time.monotonic() >= search_deadline - 20.0:
            break

        remaining = search_deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining <= 0.0:
            break

        sort_pool(pool)
        if r + 1 < len(pool):
            start_seq = pool[r + 1][1].copy()
        else:
            start_seq = best_seq.copy()

        if r > 0 and rng.random() < 0.55:
            start_seq = random_big_perturb(start_seq, rng, n)

        slice_len = min(160.0, max(25.0, remaining / float(max(1, n_rounds - r))))
        spec_end = min(search_deadline, time.monotonic() + 0.42 * slice_len)
        loc_end = min(search_deadline, time.monotonic() + slice_len)

        f1, sc1 = run_spectral(start_seq, L, n, spec_end, rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc1) and sc1 < best_fft:
            best_seq = f1.copy()
            best_fft = sc1
        add_candidate(pool, sc1, f1)

        f2, sc2 = run_local_search(f1, L, n, loc_end, rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_fft:
            best_seq = f2.copy()
            best_fft = sc2
        add_candidate(pool, sc2, f2)

        print("round=%02d best_fft=%.9f pool=%d time=%.1f" % (
            r, best_fft, len(pool), time.monotonic() - start_time), flush=True)

    sort_pool(pool)

    # Exact final validation on a few top candidates.
    exact_best_seq = None
    exact_best_score = np.inf
    hard_deadline = start_time + budget

    def try_exact(seq):
        try:
            s = evaluate_sequence(seq)
            s = float(s)
            return s
        except Exception:
            return np.inf

    # Always consider the current best FFT candidate.
    seq = [float(x) for x in best_seq]
    sc = try_exact(seq)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < exact_best_score:
        exact_best_score = sc
        exact_best_seq = seq

    # Then evaluate the top pool candidates, ordered by FFT score.
    for i in range(min(10, len(pool))):
        if time.monotonic() >= hard_deadline - 1.5:
            break
        seq = [float(x) for x in pool[i][1]]
        sc = try_exact(seq)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < exact_best_score:
            exact_best_score = sc
            exact_best_seq = seq

    if exact_best_seq is None:
        exact_best_seq = [float(x) for x in best_seq]

    print("final_exact_bound=%.9f return_len=%d" % (
        exact_best_score if np.isfinite(exact_best_score) else float("inf"),
        len(exact_best_seq)), flush=True)

    return exact_best_seq
```
